The EU's REACH regulation and similar frameworks worldwide continue to tighten limits on hazardous substances."), (28, '- **Consumer preferences** — Growing consumer awareness of indoor air quality and environmental impact is driving demand for products made with non-toxic, sustainable adhesives.'), (29, '- **Circular economy goals** — Bio-based adhesives support circular economy objectives by enabling easier recycling and composting of wood products at end of life.'), (35, "- **Bakelite's global reach** — With operations across Europe, Asia, and the Americas, Bakelite can rapidly scale Sestec's technology to serve customers worldwide."), (36, "- **Formulation expertise** — Bakelite's deep knowledge of resin chemistry enhances Sestec's protein-based formulations for new applications and performance requirements."), (37, "- **Customer relationships** — Bakelite's established relationships with major manufacturers in the wood, automotive, and construction sectors provide immediate market access for Sestec's products."), (38, '- **R&D capabilities** — Combined research resources accelerate development of next-generation bio-based adhesive solutions.'), (50, '- **Reduced carbon footprint** — Bio-based adhesives can reduce the carbon footprint of bonded wood products by 30-50% compared to petroleum-based alternatives'), (51, '- **Lower toxicity** — Protein-based adhesives eliminate formaldehyde and other hazardous volatile organic compounds (VOCs) from manufacturing and use'), (52, '- **Biodegradability** — Products bonded with bio-based adhesives can be more easily recycled or composted at end of life'), (53, '- **Renewable sourcing** — Raw materials come from renewable agricultural sources rather than finite petroleum reserves')], 'pass': False, 'section': 'main_body'}

- ❌ **bullet_points**: {'count': 26, 'lines': [(15, '1. **Fermentation**: Plant sugars (glucose from corn starch or sucrose from sugarcane) are fermented by lactic acid bacteria (*Lactobacillus* species) to produce L-lactic acid.'), (16, '2. **Cyclization**: The lactic acid is converted into lactide, a cyclic dimer intermediate.'), (17, '3. **Ring-opening polymerization (ROP)**: The lactide is polymerized into high-molecular-weight PLA using a catalyst (typically tin octoate).'), (48, '- **Excellent clarity and gloss** — ideal for transparent packaging'), (49, '- **Good stiffness** — suitable for rigid containers, cups, and cutlery'), (50, '- **Processable on standard equipment** — low barrier to adoption for converters'), (51, '- **Low carbon footprint** — 50-70% lower greenhouse gas emissions than PET or PS'), (52, '- **Industrial compostability** — certified under EN 13432 and ASTM D6400'), (53, '- **Food-safe** — FDA-approved for food contact applications'), (56, '- **Low heat resistance** — softens around 55°C, making it unsuitable for hot-fill applications or microwave use'), (57, '- **Brittle without modification** — pure PLA has low impact strength; often blended with other polymers or plasticizers'), (58, '- **Requires industrial composting** — does not degrade in home compost, soil, or marine environments within meaningful timeframes'), (59, '- **Limited barrier properties** — poor moisture and oxygen barrier compared to PET or EVOH'), (60, '- **Recycling infrastructure gaps** — PLA is recyclable in theory but rarely accepted in municipal recycling streams'), (84, '- Temperature: 58°C or higher'), (85, '- Humidity: >60% relative humidity'), (86, '- Active microbial community'), (87, '- Timeframe: 4-12 weeks for full disintegration'), (99, "- **PLA vs PHA**: PHA is marine-biodegradable and home-compostable but costs 3-5× more than PLA. The two are increasingly blended to combine PLA's processability with PHA's superior end-of-life properties."), (100, '- **PLA vs PBAT**: PBAT is fossil-based but biodegradable, flexible, and used in compostable bags. Often blended with PLA to improve flexibility and tear resistance.'), (101, '- **PLA vs Bio-PE**: Bio-PE (from sugarcane ethanol) is chemically identical to fossil PE — recyclable but not biodegradable. It competes in different applications than PLA.'), (107, '1. **Chemical recycling advances** — Companies like Carbios and Samsara Enzymes are developing enzymatic processes that depolymerize PLA back to lactic acid, enabling true circular recycling rather than downcycling.'), (109, '2. **Heat-resistant grades** — New formulations (stereocomplex PLA, nucleating agents) are pushing heat deflection temperatures above 100°C, opening applications in hot-fill and microwave-safe packaging.'), (111, '3. **Blending with PHA** — The PLA/PHA convergence is creating materials that combine the best of both: processability, cost-effectiveness, and genuine environmental biodegradability.'), (113, '4. **Capacity expansion** — New plants in China, Thailand, and potentially Europe will double global PLA capacity by 2028, driving down costs and improving availability.'), (115, '5. **Regulatory tailwinds** — Bans on single-use plastics in the EU, India, and parts of the US are creating demand for compostable alternatives where PLA is the most cost-competitive option.')], 'pass': False, 'section': 'main_body'}
